is odd we have exactly one non-zero summand which corresponds to the unique index
i^{*}
such that
(i^{*}-1)/N<1/2<i^{*}/N
, namely the unique index
i^{*}
such that
x_{T(\mathfrak{y})}
is discontinuous in
[\frac{i^{*}-1}{N},\frac{i^{*}}{N})
. Therefore

(6.13)

\begin{split}N\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|r_{T(\mathfrak{y})}\Big(\frac{i}{N}\Big)\right|^{2}&{\sf d}^{2}\left(x_{T(\mathfrak{y})}\Big(\frac{i}{N}\Big),x_{T(\mathfrak{y})}\Big(\frac{i-1}{N}\Big)\right)\\
&=N\left|r_{T(\mathfrak{y})}\Big(\frac{i^{*}}{N}\Big)\right|^{2}{\sf d}^{2}\left(x_{T(\mathfrak{y})}\Big(\frac{i^{*}}{N}\Big),x_{T(\mathfrak{y})}\Big(\frac{i^{*}-1}{N}\Big)\right)\\
&\leq N\frac{4R^{2}}{N^{2}}\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}=\frac{R^{2}\pi^{2}}{N},\end{split}

where the inequality is due to Theorem
4.5
(d) and to the fact that, by definition of
T
and Theorem
4.5
(c),

\left|r_{T(\mathfrak{y})}\Big(\frac{i^{*}}{N}\Big)\right|\leq\left|\frac{2i^{*}}{N}-1\right|\max\{r_{\mathfrak{y}}(0),r_{\mathfrak{y}}(1)\}\leq\frac{2R}{N}.

If we now plug (
6.13
) into the expression of
I_{4}^{N}
, we get the estimate
I_{4}^{N}\leq\frac{1}{N}R^{2}\pi^{2}
for
N
odd.

If instead
N
is even, there exists exactly one index
i^{*}
such that
(i^{*}-1)/N=1/2
, which implies
r_{T(\mathfrak{y})}((i^{*}-1)/N)=0
; for all the other indexes
i=0,\dots,N
,
i\neq i^{*}
, we have
{\sf d}(x_{T(\mathfrak{y})}(i/N),x_{T(\mathfrak{y})}((i-1)/N))=0
and thus
I_{4}^{N}=0
for
N
even.

Overall,

\limsup_{N\to+\infty}I_{4}^{N}\leq 0=\int_{A\setminus A_{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{1}|r_{T(\mathfrak{y})}(t)|^{2}|x_{T(\mathfrak{y})}^{\prime}(t)|^{2}_{\sf d}{\,\rm d}t{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}(\mathfrak{y}),

since
|x_{T(\mathfrak{y})}^{\prime}(t)|_{\sf d}=0
a.e. in
[0,1]
, for any
\mathfrak{y}\in A\setminus A_{\varepsilon}
.

In conclusion, combining the above estimates on
I_{1}^{N},I_{2}^{N},I_{3}^{N},I_{4}^{N}
with (
6.12
) and (
6.11
) we deduce that

\begin{split}(\mathsf{He}\nabla\mathsf{W})^{2}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})&=\liminf_{N\to+\infty}\inf\big\{\mathcal{E}_{N}(P)\,:\,P\in\mathscr{P}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1};N)\big\}\leq\liminf_{N\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}_{N}(P_{N,\varepsilon})\\
&\leq\liminf_{N\to+\infty}\big\{I_{1}^{N}+I_{2}^{N}+I_{3}^{N}+I_{4}^{N}\big\}\leq\limsup_{N\to+\infty}\big\{I_{1}^{N}+I_{2}^{N}+I_{3}^{N}+I_{4}^{N}\big\}\\
&\leq\limsup_{N\to+\infty}I_{1}^{N}+\limsup_{N\to+\infty}I_{2}^{N}+\limsup_{N\to+\infty}I_{3}^{N}+\limsup_{N\to+\infty}I_{4}^{N}\\
&\leq\int\int_{0}^{1}|r_{\mathfrak{y}}^{\prime}(t)|^{2}{\,\rm d}t{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{\varepsilon}(\mathfrak{y})+\int_{A_{\varepsilon}}\int_{0}^{1}|r_{\mathfrak{y}}(t)|^{2}|x_{\mathfrak{y}}^{\prime}(t)|^{2}_{\sf d}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}(\mathfrak{y})\\
&\quad+\int_{A^{c}}\int_{0}^{1}|r_{\mathfrak{y}}(t)|^{2}|x_{\mathfrak{y}}^{\prime}(t)|^{2}_{\sf d}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}(\mathfrak{y})+\int\int_{0}^{1}|r_{\mathfrak{y}}(t)|^{2}|x_{\mathfrak{y}}^{\prime}(t)|^{2}_{\sf d}{\,\rm d}T_{\sharp}({\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(A\setminus A_{\varepsilon}))\\
&=\int_{A_{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{A}_{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}+\int_{A^{c}}\mathcal{A}_{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}+\int\mathcal{A}_{2}{\,\rm d}T_{\sharp}({\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(A\setminus A_{\varepsilon}))\\
&=\int\mathcal{A}_{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{\varepsilon}.\end{split}

As this inequality holds true for any fixed
\varepsilon>0
, passing to the limit as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
and applying Lemma
6.2
yield

\begin{split}(\mathsf{He}\nabla\mathsf{W})^{2}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})&\leq\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\int\mathcal{A}_{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{\varepsilon}=\int\mathcal{A}_{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}=\int\int_{0}^{1}|\mathfrak{y}^{\prime}(t)|_{{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}}^{2}{\,\rm d}t{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}(\mathfrak{y})\\
&=\int_{0}^{1}|\mu_{t}^{\prime}|_{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}{\,\rm d}t={\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1}),\end{split}

thus concluding the proof.
∎

7.
{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}\leq\mathsf{He}\nabla\mathsf{W}

Aim of this section is to prove the converse inequality to (
6.1
), namely the following

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
(X,{\sf d})
be a complete and separable metric space and let
\mu_{0},\mu_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(X)
. Then

{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}_{{\sf d}}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})\leq(\mathsf{He}_{2}\nabla\mathsf{W}_{2,{\sf d}})(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})\,.

Unlike in Theorem
6.3
, notice that for this inequality we do not even require the space to be geodesic.

For ease of notation, in what follows we drop the dependence on the exponent
p=2
and on the distance
{\sf d}
in
\mathsf{He},{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}},\mathsf{W}
. Moreover, as the proof of Theorem
7.1
is rather involved, we present here the strategy. Again, we denote by
\mathcal{E}_{N}
the energy as in (
3.1
) where
{\sf c}_{1}=\mathsf{He}
and
{\sf c}_{2}=\mathsf{W}
, unless otherwise stated.
In comparison with the previous section, to prove it we need to adopt a rather opposite approach. Indeed, in Section
6
we started with a
{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}
-geodesic between
\mu_{0}
and
\mu_{1}
and by time discretization we were naturally led to an optimal family of
N
-paths between the same measures. Now we start with an optimal family of
N
-paths and we need to find a way to interpolate between them: this will first require a gluing procedure and a subsequent lifting to
\mathcal{P}({\mathrm{C}}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}})))
.

More explicitly, assuming without loss of generality that
(\mathsf{He}\nabla\mathsf{W})(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})<+\infty
, by definition of
\mathsf{He}\nabla\mathsf{W}
there exist a sequence
(N_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
and
N_{k}
-paths
(P^{\prime}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

P^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathscr{P}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1};N_{k}),\quad N_{k}\uparrow+\infty,\quad\mathcal{E}_{N_{k}}(P^{\prime}_{k})\to(\mathsf{He}\nabla\mathsf{W})^{2}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})\quad\text{ as }k\to+\infty\,.

By Proposition
5.2
(4) we can replace every
N_{k}
-path
P^{\prime}_{k}=(\mu_{0}^{k},\mu_{1}^{k},\dots,\mu_{N_{k}}^{k};\tilde{\nu}_{1}^{k},\dots,\tilde{\nu}_{N_{k}}^{k})
with
P_{k}\coloneqq(\mu_{0}^{k},\mu_{1}^{k},\dots,\mu_{N_{k}}^{k};\nu_{1}^{k},\dots,\nu_{N_{k}}^{k})
, where
\nu_{i}^{k}
is such that

\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}(\mu_{i-1}^{k},\mu_{i}^{k})=\mathsf{He}^{2}(\mu_{i-1}^{k},\nu_{i}^{k})+\mathsf{W}^{2}(\nu_{i}^{k},\mu_{i}^{k})\quad\text{ for every }1\leq i\leq N_{k},\,k\in\mathbb{N}\,.

For the new paths it clearly still holds

(7.1)

\mathcal{E}_{N_{k}}(P_{k})\to(\mathsf{He}\nabla\mathsf{W})^{2}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})\quad\text{ as }k\to+\infty\,,

so that the inequality
{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})\leq(\mathsf{He}\nabla\mathsf{W})(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})
is proven if we show that
{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})\leq\lim_{k\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}_{N_{k}}(P_{k})
. Moreover, by (
7.1
) there exists a constant
C\in(0,+\infty)
such that

(7.2)

\mathcal{E}_{N_{k}}(P_{k})\leq C\quad\text{ for every }k\in\mathbb{N}\,.

By Jensen’s inequality this implies

\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N_{k}}\sqrt{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}(\mu_{i-1}^{k},\mu_{i}^{k})}\right)^{2}\leq\mathcal{E}_{N_{k}}(P_{k})\leq C\qquad\text{ for every }k\in\mathbb{N}\,,

whence

(7.3)

\sqrt{\mu_{0}(X)}+\sum_{i=1}^{N_{k}}\sqrt{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}(\mu_{i-1}^{k},\mu_{i}^{k})}\leq\sqrt{\mu_{0}(X)}+\sqrt{C}=:\Theta\,,

so that the left-hand side is bounded by a finite positive constant independent of
k
. By (a simple adaptation of)
[
28
, Lemma 7.11]
we can thus find probability measures
{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{C}_{\Theta}[X]^{N_{k}+1})
, see (
2.6a
), such that

(7.4)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{i}^{k}\coloneqq\pi^{i-1,i}_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}\in\mathfrak{H}^{2}(\mu_{i-1}^{k},\mu_{i}^{k})\\[10.0pt]
\displaystyle{\int_{\mathfrak{C}[X,X]}\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{i}^{k}=\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}(\mu_{i-1}^{k},\mu_{i}^{k})}\end{array}\right.\quad\textrm{ for every }i=1,\dots,N_{k},\,k\in\mathbb{N}\,,

where
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}
is as in (
5.2
). The energy
\mathcal{E}_{N_{k}}
then rewrites as

(7.5)

\mathcal{E}_{N_{k}}(P_{k})=N_{k}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{k}}\int_{\mathfrak{C}[X,X]}\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{i}^{k}\,,

which resembles a discretization of the action
\mathcal{A}_{2}
, see (
4.9
). We are thus naturally led to lift
{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}
to
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathrm{AC}^{2}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}})))
, because if we prove that

(7.6)

\int\mathcal{A}_{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}\leq\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int N_{k}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{k}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}\circ({\sf e}_{\frac{i-1}{N_{k}}},{\sf e}_{\frac{i}{N_{k}}}){\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}=\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\mathcal{E}_{N_{k}}(P_{k})\,,

for some
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathrm{AC}^{2}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}})))
with
({\sf e}_{0},{\sf e}_{1})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}\in\mathfrak{H}^{2}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})
, then the desired inequality
{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})\leq(\mathsf{He}\nabla\mathsf{W})(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})
follows from the dynamical representation (
4.10
) of
{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}
.

However, for the above inequality to hold we need tightness of the measures
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}
, so that
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}\rightharpoonup{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}
for some
\pi
, and some sort of uniform convergence of the integrands towards
\mathcal{A}_{2}
. To overcome the first obstacle, we assume
X
to be compact: this is not restrictive by Proposition
7.4
. The second obstacle is instead more involved. If the supports of the measures
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}
and
\pi
only contain curves with radii uniformly bounded away from 0, then (
7.6
) holds true: the idea is to bound, as in (
7.45
),

\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}\circ({\sf e}_{\frac{i-1}{N_{k}}},{\sf e}_{\frac{i}{N_{k}}})(\mathfrak{y})\geq\left(1\wedge\frac{r_{\mathfrak{y}}(i/N_{k})}{r_{\mathfrak{y}}((i-1)/N_{k})}\right){\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{2}(x_{\mathfrak{y}}((i-1)/N_{k}),x_{\mathfrak{y}}(i/N_{k}))

and, if
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}
is concentrated between the time steps
\frac{i-1}{N_{k}}
and
\frac{i}{N_{k}}
on constant-speed geodesics, then
{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}(x_{\mathfrak{y}}((i-1)/N_{k}),x_{\mathfrak{y}}(i/N_{k}))
coincides with the rescaled metric speed
N_{k}^{-1}|\mathfrak{y}^{\prime}(t)|_{{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
. Integrating in time the right-hand side above thus yields (up to a multiplicative factor tending to 1 as
k\to+\infty
) the action
\mathcal{A}_{2}
. Handling the liminf as
k\to+\infty
when the above inequality is integrated w.r.t.
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}
is discussed in Proposition
7.6
.

Incidentally, the above discussion suggests how to actually lift
{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}
to
\mathcal{P}(\mathrm{AC}^{2}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}})))
. We define a geodesic interpolation map
\Gamma^{N_{k}}:\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N_{k}+1}\to\mathrm{AC}^{2}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}))
that sends the
(N_{k}+1)
-tuple
(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}})
into an absolutely continuous curve that, on every interval
[(i-1)/N_{k},i/N_{k}]
,
i=1,\dots,N_{k}
, is a reparametrized constant-speed geodesic connecting
\mathfrak{y}_{i-1}
and
\mathfrak{y}_{i}
with metric speed equal to
N_{k}{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathfrak{y}_{i-1},\mathfrak{y}_{i})
. Then,
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}\coloneqq\Gamma^{N_{k}}_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}
.

However, we cannot exclude the limit measure
\pi
to concentrate on curves that either start or end in
\mathfrak{o}
. This motivates a first splitting of
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}
and
\pi
. The idea, although slightly different from the one adopted in the proof of Theorem
7.1
(we will perform the splitting on
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}\coloneqq({\sf e}_{0},{\sf e}_{1})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}
and
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}\coloneqq({\sf e}_{0},{\sf e}_{1})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}
), is the following: we single out the curves that either start or end in
\mathfrak{o}
, namely
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathfrak{o}}\coloneqq\{\mathfrak{y}\in\mathrm{AC}^{2}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}))\,:\,\mathfrak{y}(0)=\mathfrak{o}\textrm{ or }\mathfrak{y}(1)=\mathfrak{o}\}
and accordingly split
\pi
into

{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}\coloneqq{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\mathfrak{D}_{\mathfrak{o}}\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{>}\coloneqq{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\mathfrak{D}_{\mathfrak{o}}^{c}\,.

Then, by Lemma
7.5
, we build measures
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{>}^{k},{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}^{k}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathrm{AC}^{2}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}})))
such that
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}={\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}^{k}+{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{>}^{k}
and
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}^{k}\rightharpoonup{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}
,
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{>}^{k}\rightharpoonup{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{>}
as
k\to+\infty
. It is not difficult to check that

(7.7)

\begin{split}\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int N_{k}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{k}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}\circ({\sf e}_{\frac{i-1}{N_{k}}},{\sf e}_{\frac{i}{N_{k}}}){\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}^{k}&\geq\int_{\mathfrak{C}[X,X]}{\sf d}_{\pi/2,\mathfrak{C}}^{2}\,{\,\rm d}({\sf e}_{0},{\sf e}_{1})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}\\
&\geq{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mathfrak{h}(({\sf e}_{0})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}),\mathfrak{h}(({\sf e}_{1})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}))\end{split}

as a consequence of the definition of the cone distance, see the first step in the proof of Theorem
7.1
.
A further splitting concerns the measures
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}_{>}
: we single out a ‘good’ set of curves
\mathfrak{D}_{N_{k}}\subset\mathfrak{D}_{\mathfrak{o}}^{c}
where it holds

\mathfrak{y}\in\mathfrak{D}_{N_{k}}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad N_{k}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{k}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}\left(\mathfrak{y}\left(\frac{i-1}{N_{k}}\right),\mathfrak{y}\left(\frac{i}{N_{k}}\right)\right)\geq{\sf d}_{\pi/2,\mathfrak{C}}^{2}(\mathfrak{y}(0),\mathfrak{y}(1))\,,

so that, setting
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}_{g}\coloneqq{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}_{>}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\mathfrak{D}_{N_{k}}
, we also get

(7.8)

\begin{split}\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int N_{k}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{k}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}\circ({\sf e}_{\frac{i-1}{N_{k}}},{\sf e}_{\frac{i}{N_{k}}}){\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{g}^{k}&\geq\int_{\mathfrak{C}[X,X]}{\sf d}_{\pi/2,\mathfrak{C}}^{2}\,{\,\rm d}({\sf e}_{0},{\sf e}_{1})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{g}\\
&\geq{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mathfrak{h}(({\sf e}_{0})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{g}),\mathfrak{h}(({\sf e}_{1})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{g})),\end{split}

where
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{g}
is the weak limit of
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{g}^{k}
as
k\to+\infty
.

It remains to handle the
energy of the plan

{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}_{b}
, that is the integral

(7.9)

\int N_{k}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{k}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}\circ({\sf e}_{\frac{i-1}{N_{k}}},{\sf e}_{\frac{i}{N_{k}}}){\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}_{b},

where
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}_{b}\coloneqq{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}_{>}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(\mathfrak{D}_{\mathfrak{o}}^{c}\setminus\mathfrak{D}_{N_{k}})
with weak limit
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{b}
as
k\to+\infty
. Recalling that if
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}_{b}
and
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{b}
are concentrated on curves with radii uniformly bounded away from zero then (
7.6
) (written for
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}_{b}
and
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{b}
) holds true, by a cut-off argument we introduce a new family of measures
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{b}^{k,m}
concentrated on curves
\mathfrak{y}
with
1/m<r_{\mathfrak{y}}(0),r_{\mathfrak{y}}(1)\leq\Theta
with a lower energy, and also satisfying

(7.10)

\lim_{m\to+\infty}{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mathfrak{h}(({\sf e}_{0})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{b}^{m}),\mathfrak{h}(({\sf e}_{1})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{b}^{m}))={\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mathfrak{h}(({\sf e}_{0})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{b}),\mathfrak{h}(({\sf e}_{1})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{b}))\,.

The cut-off makes the radii bounded away from zero at the initial and final time. We cannot avoid the radii to become arbitrarily small or vanish at intermediate times. However, we can show that if this happens, then for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
we can find a better plan
\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}}^{k,m}_{b}
(that is, with a lower energy) with the same initial and final 2-homogeneous marginals, and additionally concentrated on curves with radii uniformly bounded away from zero at all times: this is achieved by exploiting the specific form of the set
\mathfrak{D}_{N_{k}}
, the structure of geodesics on the cone, building a map
G_{N_{k}}
lifting those curves whose radii are too close to
0
(see Theorem
7.3
for the precise result), and setting
\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}}^{k,m}_{b}\coloneqq(G_{N_{k}})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{b}^{k,m}
. The latter map is constructed studying the properties of minimizers of the function

N_{k}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{k}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}(\mathfrak{y}_{i-1},\mathfrak{y}_{i})\quad\text{on the set}\quad\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N_{k}+1}\,,

and it is at the core of the arguments used in the proof of Theorem
7.1
. We can finally apply Proposition
7.4
and obtain (
7.6
) for the plan
\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}}^{k,m}_{b}
, that is

\displaystyle{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mathfrak{h}(({\sf e}_{0})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{b}^{m}),\mathfrak{h}(({\sf e}_{1})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{b}^{m}))

\displaystyle={\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mathfrak{h}(({\sf e}_{0})_{\sharp}\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}}_{b}^{m}),\mathfrak{h}(({\sf e}_{1})_{\sharp}\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}}_{b}^{m}))\leq\int\mathcal{A}_{2}{\,\rm d}\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}}^{m}_{b}

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int N_{k}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{k}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}\circ({\sf e}_{\frac{i-1}{N_{k}}},{\sf e}_{\frac{i}{N_{k}}}){\,\rm d}\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}}^{k,m}_{b}\,,

where
\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}}^{m}_{b}
is the weak limit of
\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}}^{k,m}_{b}
as
k\to+\infty
, and the first inequality follows from the dynamical formulation of
{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}
, see (
4.10
). Since, as discussed above, the energy of
\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}}^{k,m}_{b}
lower bounds the one of
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k,m}_{b}
and, in turn, this is less than the one of
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}_{b}
for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we conclude that

{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mathfrak{h}(({\sf e}_{0})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{b}^{m}),\mathfrak{h}(({\sf e}_{1})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{b}^{m}))\leq\liminf_{k\to+\infty}N_{k}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{k}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}\circ({\sf e}_{\frac{i-1}{N_{k}}},{\sf e}_{\frac{i}{N_{k}}}){\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}_{b}\quad\text{ for every }m\in\mathbb{N}.

Using (
7.10
), a final passage to the limit as
m\to+\infty
gives

(7.11)

{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mathfrak{h}(({\sf e}_{0})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{b}),\mathfrak{h}(({\sf e}_{1})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{b}))\leq\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int N_{k}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{k}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}\circ({\sf e}_{\frac{i-1}{N_{k}}},{\sf e}_{\frac{i}{N_{k}}}){\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}_{b}.

Together with (
7.5
), (
7.7
), (
7.8
), and the subadditivity of
{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}
, this yields
{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}\leq\mathsf{He}\nabla\mathsf{W}
.

7.1.
A priori estimates for the minimization problem

In this section we study,
at a discrete level
, the minimization problem associated with the inf-convolution between the Hellinger and the Wasserstein distance. We obtain some a priori bounds on the minimizers that will be crucial for the convergence results for general measures contained in the next subsection.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}_{>1}
, and
r_{0},r_{N}>0
. Let
f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\cdot):\mathbb{R}^{2N-1}\to[0,+\infty)
be the function

(7.12)

f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};r_{1},\dots,r_{N-1},d_{1},\dots,d_{N})\coloneqq N\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(|r_{i}-r_{i-1}|^{2}+r_{i}^{2}d_{i}^{2}\right).

Notice that
f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\cdot)
is clearly continuous and symmetric w.r.t. the origin in the variables
d_{i}
. Given
d\geq 0
, we also introduce the sets
\Omega_{\geq d}^{N},\Omega_{d}^{N}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2N-1}
defined as

(7.13)

\Omega_{\geq d}^{N}\coloneqq\left\{r_{i}>0,\,d_{i}\geq 0,\,\sum_{i=1}^{N}d_{i}\geq d\right\},\qquad\Omega_{d}^{N}\coloneqq\left\{r_{i}>0,\,d_{i}\geq 0,\,\sum_{i=1}^{N}d_{i}=d\right\}.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}_{>1}
and let us set
Y\coloneqq(0,+\infty)^{2}\times[0,+\infty)
. The following properties hold:

(1)

For every
(r_{0},r_{N},d)\in Y
, the set

(7.14)

\Psi(r_{0},r_{N},d)\coloneqq\mathop{\rm argmin}\limits_{\Omega_{\geq d}^{N}}f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\cdot)

is non-empty.

(2)

For every
(r_{0},r_{N},d)\in Y
, if

\min_{\Omega_{\geq d}^{N}}f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\cdot)\leq|r_{0}-r_{N}|^{2}+r_{0}r_{N}(d\wedge\pi/2)^{2}

then

(7.15)

\Psi(r_{0},r_{N},d)\subset\left[\Big(1-\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)r_{0}\wedge r_{N},r_{0}\vee r_{N}\right]^{N-1}\times\left[0,\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\frac{\pi}{2-\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{\sqrt{r_{0}r_{N}}}{r_{0}\wedge r_{N}}\right]^{N}\,.

(3)

There exists a Borel measurable map
U_{N}:Y\to\mathbb{R}^{2N-1}
such that
U_{N}(r_{0},r_{N},d)\in\Psi(r_{0},r_{N},d)\cap\Omega_{d}^{N}
for every
(r_{0},r_{N},d)\in Y.

Proof.

For every
r_{0},r_{N}>0
, the function
f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\cdot)
is increasing in the
d_{i}
-components in
[0,+\infty)
, hence it is immediate to see that the minimization problems of
f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\cdot)
on
\Omega_{\geq d}^{N}
or on
\Omega_{d}^{N}
are equivalent (in the sense that they have the same minimizers and the same minimal value); we thus restrict ourselves to study the problem in the latter set.
For every
(r_{0},r_{N},0)
in the Borel set
Y_{0}\coloneqq(0,+\infty)^{2}\times\{0\}
, setting
r_{i}\coloneqq r_{0}+i/N(r_{N}-r_{0})
for
i=1,\dots,N-1
, it is also immediate to see that the point
p=(r_{1},\dots,r_{N-1},0,\dots,0)\in\Omega_{0}^{N}
is the only minimizer of
f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\cdot)
in
\Omega_{0}^{N}
and all the statements trivially hold with
Y
replaced by
Y_{0}
. It is thus not restrictive to assume
d>0
in the following, and construct a Borel measurable map
U_{N}:Y\setminus Y_{0}\to\mathbb{R}^{2N-1}
.

(1) We fix
(r_{0},r_{N},d)\in Y\setminus Y_{0}
, we let

\overline{\Omega}_{d}^{N}=\left\{r_{i}\geq 0,\,d_{i}\geq 0,\,\sum_{i=1}^{N}d_{i}=d\right\}

be the closure of
\Omega_{d}^{N}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2N-1}
, and we set

(7.16)

C\coloneqq\left[0,\frac{N(r_{0}^{2}+r_{N}^{2})^{1/2}}{d}\right]^{N-1}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2N-1},\qquad K\coloneqq\overline{\Omega}_{d}^{N}\cap C\,.

Since
f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\cdot)
is continuous and
K
is compact,
f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\cdot)
has at least a minimum point in
K
. For every
z=(r_{1},\dots,r_{N-1,}d_{1},\dots,d_{N})\in\overline{\Omega}_{d}^{N}\cap C^{c}
let
j\in\{1,\dots,N\}
be an index such that
d_{j}\geq d/N
(notice that such an index
j
must exist); we have

f(z)\geq Nr_{j}^{2}d_{j}^{2}>\frac{N^{3}}{d^{2}}(r_{0}^{2}+r_{N}^{2})\frac{d^{2}}{N^{2}}=N(r_{0}^{2}+r_{N}^{2})=f(c)\,,

where
c=(0,\dots,0,d,0,\dots,0)\in K
. Thus

\mathop{\rm argmin}\limits_{K}f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\cdot)=\mathop{\rm argmin}\limits_{\overline{\Omega}_{d}^{N}}f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\cdot).

To conclude, we show that any minimizer
z^{*}\coloneqq(r_{1}^{*},\dots,r^{*}_{N-1},d^{*}_{1},\dots,d^{*}_{N})
of
f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\cdot)
in
\overline{\Omega}_{d}^{N}
is such that
r^{*}_{i}>0
for every
i=1,\dots,N-1
, so that

(7.17)

\mathop{\rm argmin}\limits_{K}f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\cdot)=\mathop{\rm argmin}\limits_{\Omega_{d}^{N}}f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\cdot).

Indeed, for any
z\coloneqq(r_{1},\dots,r_{N-1},d_{1},\dots,d_{N})\in\overline{\Omega}_{d}^{N}
the map

t\mapsto f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};t,r_{2},\dots,r_{N-1},d_{1},\dots,d_{N})

is strictly decreasing in
[0,r_{0}/(2+d_{1}^{2}))
as an immediate consequence of its expression, which implies
r_{1}^{*}>0
. Moreover, if
z\coloneqq(r_{1},\dots,r_{N-1},d_{1},\dots,d_{N})\in\overline{\Omega}_{d}^{N}
is such that
r_{j}>0
for every
1\leq j\leq i-1
, with
1<i<N
, then the map

t\mapsto f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};r_{1},\dots,r_{i-1},t,r_{i+1},\dots,r_{N-1},d_{1},\dots,d_{N})

is strictly decreasing in
[0,r_{i-1}/(2+d_{i}^{2}))
which implies
r^{*}_{i}>0
and concludes the proof of the first point of the statement.

(2)
We fix
(r_{0},r_{N},d)\in Y\setminus Y_{0}
; notice that
f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\cdot)
is a smooth function and the constraint
\sum_{i=1}^{N}d_{i}=d
is admissible, thus every
z=(r_{1},\dots,r_{N-1},d_{1},\dots,d_{N})\in\Psi(r_{0},r_{N},d)
satisfies the following system of equations given by the Lagrange multipliers theorem (here we also used the symmetry in the variables
d_{i}
in order to avoid an additional constraint for
d_{i}\geq 0
):

\begin{cases}r_{i}\left(2+d_{i}^{2}\right)=r_{i-1}+r_{i+1}\qquad&\forall i=1,\dots,N-1,\\
2r_{i}^{2}d_{i}=\lambda_{N}\qquad&\forall i=1,\dots,N,\\
\sum_{i=1}^{N}d_{i}=d\,,\end{cases}

where
\lambda_{N}
is the Lagrange multiplier. By the equations in the first line of the system we infer
2r_{i}\leq r_{i-1}+r_{i+1}
, which implies that one (and only one) of the two following situations occurs:

•

the map
i:\{0,\dots,N\}\to\mathbb{R}
,
i\mapsto r_{i}
is monotone (increasing or decreasing);

•

there exists an index
j\in\{1,\dots,N-1\}
such that the map
i\mapsto r_{i}
is monotone decreasing in
\{0,\ldots,j\}
and monotone increasing in
\{j,\dots,N\}
. In particular,
r_{j}=\min\{r_{i}:i=1,\ldots,N-1\}
.

In the first case it is obvious that
r_{i}\in[(1-\pi/4)r_{0}\wedge r_{N},r_{0}\vee r_{N}]
for every
i=1,\dots,N-1
.

In the second case we reason as follows: it is clear that
r_{i}\leq r_{0}\vee r_{N}
for every
i=1,\dots,N-1
; moreover, thanks to the upper bound in the assumption and Jensen’s inequality we have

(7.18)

\displaystyle|r_{0}-r_{N}|^{2}+r_{0}r_{N}(d\wedge\pi/2)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\min_{\Omega_{d}^{N}}f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};z)\geq N\sum_{i=1}^{N}|r_{i}-r_{i-1}|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}|r_{i}-r_{i-1}|\right)^{2}=(r_{0}+r_{N}-2r_{j})^{2},

whence

(7.19)

r_{j}\geq\frac{r_{0}+r_{N}-\sqrt{r_{0}^{2}+r_{N}^{2}+r_{0}r_{N}(\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}-2)}}{2}.

Introducing the function
g:[1,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
,
g(t)=1+t-\sqrt{1+t^{2}+t(\pi^{2}/4-2)}
, by elementary considerations one can prove that
g
is minimized at
t=1
with value
g(1)=2-\pi/2
. This fact, together with the bound (
7.19
), gives that

(7.20)

r_{j}\geq\Big(1-\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)r_{0}\wedge r_{N}\,.

It remains to prove that

d_{k}\coloneqq\max\{d_{i}:i=1,\ldots,N\}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\frac{\pi}{2-\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{\sqrt{r_{0}r_{N}}}{r_{0}\wedge r_{N}}\,.

To prove this bound we can reason as in the proof of (
7.18
) to get

|r_{0}-r_{N}|^{2}+r_{0}r_{N}(d\wedge\pi/2)^{2}\geq(r_{0}-r_{N})^{2}+N\sum_{i=1}^{N}r_{i}^{2}d_{i}^{2}

which implies, using (
7.20
),

r_{0}r_{N}\frac{\pi^{2}}{4}\geq N\left(1-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)^{2}(r_{0}\wedge r_{N})^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}d_{i}^{2}\geq N\left(1-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)^{2}(r_{0}\wedge r_{N})^{2}d_{k}^{2}\,,

which gives the desired bound on
d_{k}
.

(3) The Borel map
U_{N}:Y\setminus Y_{0}\to\mathbb{R}^{2N-1}
is constructed as a selection of the multi-valued map
\Psi:(0,+\infty)^{3}\to 2^{\mathbb{R}^{2N-1}}
defined in (
7.14
). In order to ensure the Borel measurability, we appeal to
[
5
, 6.9.3]
and we have to show that for every
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{2N-1}
open the set
\hat{\Psi}(U)
is Borel, where we have introduced the map

\hat{\Psi}:2^{\mathbb{R}^{2N-1}}\to 2^{(0,+\infty)^{3}},\qquad\hat{\Psi}(U)\coloneqq\{(r_{0},r_{N},d)\in(0,+\infty)^{3}\ :\ \Psi(r_{0},r_{N},d)\cap U\neq\emptyset\}\,.

It sufficient to show that
\hat{\Psi}(C)
is closed for every
C\subset\mathbb{R}^{2N-1}
closed, since every open set
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{2N-1}
is a countable union of closed sets
\cup_{n}C_{n}=U
and
\hat{\Psi}(\cup_{n}C_{n})=\cup_{n}\hat{\Psi}(C_{n})
. So let
y^{j}\coloneqq(r^{j}_{0},r^{j}_{N},d^{j})
be a sequence of points in
\hat{\Psi}(C)
such that
y^{j}\to y\coloneqq(r_{0},r_{N},d)\in(0,+\infty)^{3}
; we aim to show that
y\in\hat{\Psi}(C)
. Since
(y^{j})_{j}
is converging, we can assume it is contained in a compact set
K^{\prime}=[a,b]^{3}\subset(0,+\infty)^{3}
and from the proof of point
(1)
we know that every sequence
(x^{j})_{j}
such that
x^{j}\in\Psi(y^{j})\cap C
for every
j
is contained in a compact set
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{2N-1}
of the form (recalling (
7.16
) and (
7.17
))

K=\left\{(r_{1},\dots,r_{N-1},d_{1},\dots,d_{N})\,:\,r_{i}\in\left[0,\frac{\sqrt{2}Nb}{a}\right],\,d_{i}\geq 0,\,\sum_{i=1}^{N}d_{i}\in[a,b]\right\}\,.

Up to a non-relabeled subsequence, we can thus assume that
x^{j}
is converging to some
x\in C
; let
p=(s_{1},\dots,s_{N-1},c_{1},\dots,c_{N})\in\Omega_{d}^{N}
. We can consider the sequence
p^{j}\coloneqq(s_{1},\dots,s_{N-1},c_{1}d^{j}/d,\dots,c_{N}d^{j}/d)\in\Omega_{d^{j}}^{N}
so that
p^{j}\to p
and

f_{N}(r_{0}^{j},r_{N}^{j};x^{j})\leq f_{N}(r_{0}^{j},r_{N}^{j};p^{j})

since
x^{j}\in\Psi(y^{j})
. Using the continuity of
f_{N}(\cdot,\cdot\,;\cdot):\mathbb{R}^{2N+1}\to[0,+\infty)
, passing to the limit in the last inequality we obtain

f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};x)\leq f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};p)

which shows that
x\in\Psi(y)
due to the arbitrariness of
p
, so that
y\in\hat{\Psi}(C)
.
∎

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
. We introduce the function
\mathsf{H}_{N}:\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1}\to[0,+\infty)
defined as

(7.21)

\mathsf{H}_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N})\coloneqq N\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}(\mathfrak{y}_{i-1},\mathfrak{y}_{i})\,.

We also introduce

(7.22)

\tilde{{\sf d}}_{\mathfrak{C}}:\mathfrak{C}[X]^{2}\to[0,+\infty),\qquad\tilde{{\sf d}}^{2}_{\mathfrak{C}}([x,r],[y,s])\coloneqq|r-s|^{2}+rs({\sf d}(x,y)\wedge\pi/2)^{2}\,.

Notice that
\tilde{{\sf d}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
is well defined on
\mathfrak{C}[X]^{2}
with

(7.23)

\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}(\mathfrak{o},[y,s])=\tilde{{\sf d}}^{2}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathfrak{o},[y,s])=s^{2}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}([x,r],\mathfrak{o})=\tilde{{\sf d}}^{2}_{\mathfrak{C}}([x,r],\mathfrak{o})=r^{2}.

Using formulation (
2.8
), it follows immediately that
\tilde{{\sf d}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\geq\mathsf{d}_{\pi/2,\mathfrak{C}}
where
\mathsf{d}_{\pi/2,\mathfrak{C}}
was defined in (
2.7
).

In the next statement

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\pi^{i}&:&\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1}&\to&\mathfrak{C}[X]\\[5.0pt]
&&(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N})&\mapsto&\mathfrak{y}_{i}\end{array}\,,\qquad i=0,\dots,N

denote the projection maps on the cone and
\pi^{i,j}\coloneqq(\pi^{i},\pi^{j})
,
0\leq i<j\leq N
. We use the notation
\mathcal{B}(Z)
for the Borel sigma-algebra of a complete and separable metric space
Z
, and
\mathcal{B}(Z)^{*}
for the sigma-algebra of
universally measurable
subsets of
Z
. We refer the reader to Appendix
A
for the details about universally measurable subsets.

Theorem 7.3
.

Let
(X,{\sf d})
be a complete, separable and geodesic metric space, and let
N\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
.
There exists a map
G_{N}:\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1}\to\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1}
which is
\mathcal{B}(\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1})^{*}
-
\mathcal{B}(\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1})
measurable such that

(7.24)

\pi^{0}\circ G_{N}=\pi^{0},\quad\pi^{N}\circ G_{N}=\pi^{N}\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathsf{H}_{N}\circ G_{N}\leq\mathsf{H}_{N}\ \textrm{on}\ \mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1},

and such that, on the set
\mathcal{D}_{N}\coloneqq\{\mathsf{H}_{N}<\tilde{{\sf d}}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{2}\circ\pi^{0,N}\}\subset\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1}
, it holds

(7.25)

\mathsf{r}\circ\pi^{i}\circ G_{N}\in\left[\Big(1-\frac{\pi}{4}\Big)\Big[(\mathsf{r}\circ\pi^{0})\wedge(\mathsf{r}\circ\pi^{N})\Big],(\mathsf{r}\circ\pi^{0})\vee(\mathsf{r}\circ\pi^{N})\right]

for every
i=0,\dots,N,
and

(7.26)

\mathsf{d}(\mathsf{x}(\pi^{i-1}\circ G_{N}),\mathsf{x}(\pi^{i}\circ G_{N}))\in\left[0,\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\frac{\pi}{2-\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{\sqrt{(\mathsf{r}\circ\pi^{0})(\mathsf{r}\circ\pi^{N})}}{(\mathsf{r}\circ\pi^{0})\wedge(\mathsf{r}\circ\pi^{N})}\right]

for every
i=1,\dots,N
, where
\mathsf{H}_{N}
is as in (
7.21
).

Proof.

We start by noticing that for every
(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N})\in\mathcal{D}_{N}
we have
\mathfrak{y}_{i}=[x_{i},r_{i}]\neq\mathfrak{o}
for
i=0,N
. Indeed, by contradiction, if
(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N})\in\mathcal{D}_{N}
with
[x_{N},r_{N}]=\mathfrak{o}
(the case
[x_{0},r_{0}]=\mathfrak{o}
is similar), using (
7.23
) and Jensen’s inequality we have

r_{0}^{2}=\tilde{{\sf d}}^{2}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{o})>N\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}(\mathfrak{y}_{i-1},\mathfrak{y}_{i})\geq N\sum_{i=1}^{N}|r_{i-1}-r_{i}|^{2}\geq\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}|r_{i-1}-r_{i}|\right)^{2}\geq r^{2}_{0}\,,

which yields the contradiction.

For
\mathfrak{C}[X]\ni\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N}\neq\mathfrak{o}
we define the function
\mathsf{H}_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N};\cdot):\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N-1}\to[0,+\infty)
as
\mathsf{H}_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N};\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N-1})=\mathsf{H}_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N})
, and the sets

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}[X]_{\mathfrak{o}}^{N-1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{(\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N-1})\in\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N-1}\ :\ \exists i=1,\dots,N-1\text{ such that }\mathfrak{y}_{i}=\mathfrak{o}\}\,,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}[X]_{>0}^{N-1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{(\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N-1})\in\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N-1}\ :\ \mathfrak{y}_{i}\neq\mathfrak{o}\ \forall i=1,\dots,N-1\}\,.

We claim that:

(7.27)

\displaystyle\text{if $\mathfrak{C}[X]\ni\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N}\neq\mathfrak{o}$, $\forall(\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N-1})$}

\displaystyle\text{$\in\mathfrak{C}[X]_{\mathfrak{o}}^{N-1}$ $\exists(\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{N-1})\in\mathfrak{C}[X]_{>0}^{N-1}$ such that}

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N};\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N-1})

\displaystyle>\mathsf{H}_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N};\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{N-1}).

To show this, we firstly notice that for every
[x,r],[y,s]\in\mathfrak{C}[X]
with
r>0
, we can find a point
\mathfrak{o}\neq[z,t]\in\mathfrak{C}[X]
such that

(7.28)

r^{2}+s^{2}=\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}([x,r],\mathfrak{o})+\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}(\mathfrak{o},[y,s])>\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}([x,r],[z,t])+\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}([z,t],[y,s]).

Indeed, using the explicit expression of
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}
it is easy to see that if
s=0
we can take
z=x
and any
t
satisfying
0<t<r
.
If
s>0
then we can take
z=y
and any
t
satisfying
0<t<2\frac{r+s}{2+{\sf d}^{2}(x,y)}
.

So let
\mathfrak{y}_{0}=[x_{0},r_{0}]
and
\mathfrak{y}_{N}=[x_{N},r_{N}]
with
r_{0},r_{N}>0
,
(\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N-1})\in\mathfrak{C}[X]_{\mathfrak{o}}^{N-1}
, and we now prove claim (
7.27
) by induction on
N
. The case
N=2
is a direct consequence of (
7.28
). Let us assume the result true for
N-1\in\mathbb{N},N>2
, and prove the case
N
.
If
r_{1}>0
we can apply the induction assumption to the function
\mathsf{H}_{N-1}(\mathfrak{y}_{1},\mathfrak{y}_{N};\cdot)
to infer the existence of
(\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{2},\dots,\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{N-1})\in\mathfrak{C}[X]_{>0}^{N-1}
such that

\mathsf{H}_{N-1}(\mathfrak{y}_{1},\mathfrak{y}_{N};\mathfrak{y}_{2},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N-1})>\mathsf{H}_{N-1}(\mathfrak{y}_{1},\mathfrak{y}_{N};\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{2},\dots,\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{N-1}).

It is then immediate to see that
(\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{N-1})=(\mathfrak{y}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{2},\dots,\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{N-1})\in\mathfrak{C}[X]_{>0}^{N-1}
satisfies the claim (
7.27
).

In the case
r_{1}=0
we firstly apply (
7.28
) to replace
\mathfrak{y}_{1}
with a point
\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{1}=[z,t]\neq\mathfrak{o}
such that

\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{o})+\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}(\mathfrak{o},\mathfrak{y}_{2})>\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{1})+\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}(\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{1},\mathfrak{y}_{2})\,,

and then to conclude we reason as above using the induction assumption on
\mathsf{H}_{N-1}(\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{1},\mathfrak{y}_{N};\cdot)
.

Now, given
\mathfrak{y}_{0}=[x_{0},r_{0}]
and
\mathfrak{y}_{N}=[x_{N},r_{N}]
with
r_{0},r_{N}>0
, we let
d\coloneqq{\sf d}(x_{0},x_{N})
and we define a map
T_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N};\cdot):\Omega_{d}^{N}\to\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N-1}
(
\Omega_{d}^{N}
being defined in (
7.13
)) as
T_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N};r_{1},\dots,r_{N-1},d_{1},\dots,d_{N})\coloneqq(\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N-1})
where for
i=1,\dots,N-1
we have set
\mathsf{r}(\mathfrak{y}_{i})\coloneqq r_{i}
and

\mathsf{x}(\mathfrak{y}_{i})\coloneqq\begin{cases}\mathsf{x}(\mathfrak{y}_{0})\quad&\text{ if }d=0,\\
\Gamma^{X}(x_{0},x_{N})\left(\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{i}d_{j}}{d}\right)\quad&\text{ if }d>0,\end{cases}

with
\Gamma^{X}
being the
\mathcal{B}(X\times X)^{*}-\mathcal{B}({\mathrm{C}}([0,1];(X,{\sf d})))
measurable geodesic selection map constructed in Theorem
A.2
. Notice that
{\sf d}(\mathsf{x}(\mathfrak{y}_{i-1}),\mathsf{x}(\mathfrak{y}_{i}))=d_{i}
for every
i=1,\dots,N.
Let
U_{N}:Y\to\mathbb{R}^{2N-1}
be the Borel measurable map defined in Lemma
7.2
, with
Y=(0,+\infty)^{2}\times[0,+\infty)
, and we define
G_{N}:\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1}\to\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1}
as

(7.29)

G_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N})\coloneqq\begin{cases}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N})\quad&\textrm{on }\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{N},\\
\left(\mathfrak{y}_{0},T_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N};U_{N}(\mathsf{r}(\mathfrak{y}_{0}),\mathsf{r}(\mathfrak{y}_{N}),{\sf d}(\mathsf{x}(\mathfrak{y}_{0}),\mathsf{x}(\mathfrak{y}_{N})))),\mathfrak{y}_{N}\right)\ &\textrm{on }\mathcal{D}_{N}.\end{cases}

Since
\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N}\neq\mathfrak{o}
in
\mathcal{D}_{N}
and since
U_{N}(r_{0},r_{N},d)
belongs to
\Omega^{N}_{d}
, we have that
G_{N}
is well defined. By definition
\pi^{0}\circ G_{N}=\pi^{0}
and
\pi^{N}\circ G_{N}=\pi^{N}
. Let us prove that
\mathsf{H}_{N}\circ G_{N}\leq\mathsf{H}_{N}
on
\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1}
: this is clear on
\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{N}
, so it remains to prove that for every
(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N})\in\mathcal{D}_{N}

(7.30)

\mathsf{H}_{N}\big(\mathfrak{y}_{0},T_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N};U_{N}(\mathsf{r}(\mathfrak{y}_{0}),\mathsf{r}(\mathfrak{y}_{N}),{\sf d}(\mathsf{x}(\mathfrak{y}_{0}),\mathsf{x}(\mathfrak{y}_{N})))),\mathfrak{y}_{N}\big)\leq\mathsf{H}_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N}).

As a consequence of claim (
7.27
) we already know that there exists
(\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{N-1})\in\mathfrak{C}[X]_{>0}^{N-1}
such that

\mathsf{H}_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{N-1},\mathfrak{y}_{N})\leq\mathsf{H}_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N-1},\mathfrak{y}_{N})\,.

Let
\tilde{r}_{i}\coloneqq\mathsf{r}(\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{i})
for
i=1,\dots,N-1
and
\tilde{d}_{i}\coloneqq{\sf d}(\mathsf{x}(\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{i-1}),\mathsf{x}(\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{i}))
for
i=1,\dots,N,
where we have put
\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{0}\coloneqq\mathfrak{y}_{0}
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{N}\coloneqq\mathfrak{y}_{N}
. Notice that
(\tilde{r}_{1},\dots,\tilde{r}_{N-1},\tilde{d}_{1},\dots,\tilde{d}_{N})\in\Omega^{N}_{\geq d}
since
{\sf d}
satisfies the triangle inequality. Using the notation
f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\cdot)
for the function defined in (
7.12
), by definition of
f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\cdot),\mathsf{H}_{N}
and
U_{N}
, it holds

\begin{split}\mathsf{H}_{N}&\big(\mathfrak{y}_{0},T_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N};U_{N}(\mathsf{r}(\mathfrak{y}_{0}),\mathsf{r}(\mathfrak{y}_{N}),{\sf d}(\mathsf{x}(\mathfrak{y}_{0}),\mathsf{x}(\mathfrak{y}_{N})))),\mathfrak{y}_{N}\big)=f_{N}\big(r_{0},r_{N};U_{N}(r_{0},r_{N},d)\big)\\
&\leq f_{N}(r_{0},r_{N};\tilde{r}_{1},\dots,\tilde{r}_{N-1},\tilde{d}_{1},\dots,\tilde{d}_{N})\\
&=\mathsf{H}_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{1},\dots,\tilde{\mathfrak{y}}_{N-1},\mathfrak{y}_{N})\leq\mathsf{H}_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N-1},\mathfrak{y}_{N})\end{split}

which gives (
7.30
).

The bounds (
7.25
) and (
7.26
) are true by construction, as a consequence of the corresponding bounds contained in (
7.15
).

It remains to show that
G_{N}
is
\mathcal{B}(\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1})^{*}
-
\mathcal{B}(\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1})
measurable. Notice that
\mathcal{D}_{N}
is a Borel subset of
\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1}
, so that it is sufficient to show that the map

(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N})\mapsto\left(\mathfrak{y}_{0},T_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N};U_{N}(\mathsf{r}(\mathfrak{y}_{0}),\mathsf{r}(\mathfrak{y}_{N}),{\sf d}(\mathsf{x}(\mathfrak{y}_{0}),\mathsf{x}(\mathfrak{y}_{N})))),\mathfrak{y}_{N}\right)

is
\mathcal{B}(\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1})^{*}
-
\mathcal{B}(\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N-1})
measurable, and this is a consequence of the
\mathcal{B}(\mathfrak{C}[X]^{2})^{*}
-
\mathcal{B}(\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N+1})
measurability of

(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N})\mapsto T_{N}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N};U_{N}(\mathsf{r}(\mathfrak{y}_{0}),\mathsf{r}(\mathfrak{y}_{N}),{\sf d}(\mathsf{x}(\mathfrak{y}_{0}),\mathsf{x}(\mathfrak{y}_{N})))).

The latter follows from the fact that
\mathcal{B}
-
\mathcal{B}
measurable maps and
\mathcal{B}^{*}
-
\mathcal{B}
measurable maps are all
\mathcal{B}^{*}
-
\mathcal{B}^{*}
measurable (see (
A.1
)) and that all the involved maps posses such measurability, as a consequence of Theorem
A.2
and Lemma
7.2
.
∎

7.2.
Auxiliary results

This subsection contains some of the auxiliary results mentioned in the strategy outline at the beginning of the section. The first result allows us to reduce, without loss of generality, the proof of Theorem
7.1
to the compact case. This additional assumption will contribute to ensure tightness of the projections
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}\coloneqq\pi^{0,N_{k}}_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}
as well as of the liftings
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k}
.

Proposition 7.4
.

Assume that, whenever
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
K_{m}\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}
is a convex and compact subset and
\nu_{0},\nu_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(K_{m})
, we have

(\mathsf{He}_{2,m}\nabla\mathsf{W}_{2,{\sf d}_{m}})(\nu_{0},\nu_{1})\geq{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}_{{\sf d}_{m}}(\nu_{0},\nu_{1}),

being
{\sf d}_{m}
the (restriction to
K_{m}
of the) distance induced by the infinity norm in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
\mathsf{He}_{2,m}
the Hellinger distance in
\mathcal{M}_{+}(K_{m})
.
Then, if
(X,{\sf d})
is a complete and separable metric space, it also holds

(\mathsf{He}_{2}\nabla\mathsf{W}_{2,{\sf d}})(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})\geq{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}_{{\sf d}}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})\quad\text{ for every }\mu_{0},\mu_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(X).

Proof.

Let
\iota:(X,{\sf d})\to(\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}),{\sf d}_{\infty})
be the Kuratowski embedding
[
22
, Proposition 1.2.12]
, where
{\sf d}_{\infty}
is the distance induced by the
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
norm in
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N})
. For every
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we define the sets

\displaystyle C_{m}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{(a_{i})_{i}\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}):a_{i}=0\text{ for every }i>m\text{ and }a_{i}\in[-m,m]\text{ for every }i\leq m\}\subset\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}),

\displaystyle K_{m}

\displaystyle\coloneqq[-m,m]^{m}\subset\mathbb{R}^{m},

and the maps
\iota_{m}:C_{m}\to K_{m}
and
A_{m}:\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N})\to\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N})
as

\displaystyle\iota_{m}((a_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}})

\displaystyle\coloneqq(a_{1},\dots,a_{m}),\quad

\displaystyle(a_{i})_{i}\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}),

\displaystyle A_{m}((a_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}})

\displaystyle\coloneqq(-m\vee a_{1}\wedge m,-m\vee a_{2}\wedge m,\dots,-m\vee a_{m}\wedge m,0,0,\dots),\quad

\displaystyle(a_{i})_{i}\in\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N}).

Note that
\iota
is an isometry from
(X,{\sf d})
to
(\ell^{\infty},{\sf d}_{\infty})
,
\iota_{m}
is an isometry from
(C_{m},{\sf d}_{\infty})
to
(K_{m},{\sf d}_{m})
and
A_{m}
is a
1
-Lipschitz map w.r.t.
{\sf d}_{\infty}
. We finally define
\mathsf{T}_{m}:\mathcal{M}_{+}(X)\to\mathcal{M}_{+}(K_{m})
as

\mathsf{T}_{m}(\mu)\coloneqq(\iota_{m}\circ A_{m}\circ\iota)_{\sharp}\mu,\quad\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(X).

By Lemma
4.6
,
\mathsf{T}_{m}
is a contraction, both w.r.t. the Wasserstein and the Hellinger distances, in the sense that, for every
\mu_{0},\mu_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(X)
, it holds

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}_{2,{\sf d}_{m}}(\mathsf{T}_{m}(\mu_{0}),\mathsf{T}_{m}(\mu_{1}))

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{W}_{2,{\sf d}}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1}),

\displaystyle\mathsf{He}_{2,m}(\mathsf{T}_{m}(\mu_{0}),\mathsf{T}_{m}(\mu_{1}))

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{He}_{2}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1}).

Let now
\mu_{0},\mu_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(X)
and let
P_{k}\in\mathscr{P}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1};N_{k})
with
N_{k}\to+\infty
be a sequence of paths such that

\mathcal{E}_{N_{k}}(P_{k})\to(\mathsf{He}_{2}\nabla\mathsf{W}_{2})^{2}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1}),

where
\mathcal{E}_{N_{k}}
is as in (
3.1
) with
{\sf c}_{1}=\mathsf{He}_{2}
and
{\sf c}_{2}=\mathsf{W}_{2,{\sf d}}
. If
P_{k}=(\mu_{0}^{k},\mu_{1}^{k},\dots,\mu_{N_{k}}^{k};\nu_{1}^{k},\dots,\nu_{N_{k}}^{k})
, we define, for every
m,k\in\mathbb{N}
, the paths

P_{k}^{m}\coloneqq(\mathsf{T}_{m}(\mu_{0}^{k}),\mathsf{T}_{m}(\mu_{1}^{k}),\dots,\mathsf{T}_{m}(\mu_{N_{k}}^{k});\mathsf{T}_{m}(\nu_{1}^{k}),\dots,\mathsf{T}_{m}(\nu_{N_{k}}^{k}))\in\mathscr{P}(\mathsf{T}_{m}(\mu_{0}),\mathsf{T}_{m}(\mu_{1});N_{k}).

By the contractivity of
\mathsf{T}_{m}
, we deduce that

\mathcal{E}^{m}_{N_{k}}(P_{k}^{m})\leq\mathcal{E}_{N_{k}}(P_{k})\quad k,m\in\mathbb{N},

where
\mathcal{E}_{N_{k}}^{m}
is as in (
3.1
) with
{\sf c}_{1}=\mathsf{He}_{2,m}
and
{\sf c}_{2}=\mathsf{W}_{2,{\sf d}_{m}}
. We thus have, for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
, that

\displaystyle(\mathsf{He}_{2}\nabla\mathsf{W}_{2,{\sf d}})^{2}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})

\displaystyle=\lim_{k}\mathcal{E}_{N_{k}}(P_{k})\geq\liminf_{k}\mathcal{E}^{m}_{N_{k}}(P_{k}^{m})

\displaystyle\geq(\mathsf{He}_{2,m}\nabla\mathsf{W}_{2,{\sf d}_{m}})^{2}(\mathsf{T}_{m}(\mu_{0}),\mathsf{T}_{m}(\mu_{1}))\geq{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}_{{\sf d}_{m}}(\mathsf{T}_{m}(\mu_{0}),\mathsf{T}_{m}(\mu_{1})),

where the last inequality holds by assumption.
Applying Proposition
4.7
to
X\coloneqq C_{m}
,
{\sf d}_{X}={\sf d}_{\infty}
,
Y\coloneqq K_{m}
,
{\sf d}_{Y}={\sf d}_{m}
and
\iota\coloneqq\iota_{m}
, and setting

\mu_{i}^{m}\coloneqq(A_{m}\circ\iota)_{\sharp}\mu_{i},\quad i=0,1,

we see that

{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}_{{\sf d}_{m}}(\mathsf{T}_{m}(\mu_{0}),\mathsf{T}_{m}(\mu_{1}))={\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}_{{\sf d}_{m}}((\iota_{m})_{\sharp}\mu_{0}^{m},(\iota_{m})_{\sharp}\mu_{1}^{m})={\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}_{{\sf d}_{\infty}}(\mu_{0}^{m},\mu_{1}^{m})\quad m\in\mathbb{N}.

We deduce that

(7.31)

(\mathsf{He}_{2}\nabla\mathsf{W}_{2,{\sf d}})(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})\geq{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}_{{\sf d}_{\infty}}(\mu_{0}^{m},\mu_{1}^{m})\quad\text{ for every }m\in\mathbb{N}.

Now we note that
\mu_{i}^{m}\rightharpoonup\iota_{\sharp}\mu_{i}
,
i=0,1
, as a consequence of the pointwise convergence (and continuity) of
A_{m}
to the identity in
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N})
. Passing to the
\liminf
as
m\to+\infty
the inequality (
7.31
) using the lower semicontinuity of
{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}
coming from Theorem
4.3
(2), we deduce that

(\mathsf{He}_{2}\nabla\mathsf{W}_{2,{\sf d}})(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})\geq{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}_{{\sf d}_{\infty}}(\iota_{\sharp}\mu_{0},\iota_{\sharp}\mu_{1}).

A further application of Proposition
4.7
with
X
,
{\sf d}_{X}={\sf d}
,
Y=\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{N})
,
{\sf d}_{Y}={\sf d}_{\infty}
and
\iota
, gives the desired conclusion.
∎

In the second lemma we show that if we are given a weakly convergent sequence of measures
(\mu_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and a splitting of the limit measure
\mu
, then we can find a ‘consistent’ splitting for each
\mu_{n}
. This fact will be pivotal in the first step of the proof of Theorem
7.1
, see p.
7.3
.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
(Y,{\sf d})
be a metric space and
\mu,\mu_{n}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(Y)
be such that
\mu_{n}\rightharpoonup\mu
as
n\to+\infty
. Assume that
\mu=\nu^{1}+\nu^{2}
with
\nu^{1},\nu^{2}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(Y)
. Then there exist a subsequence
(n_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
,
n_{k}\uparrow+\infty
as
k\to+\infty
, and
\nu_{k}^{1},\nu_{k}^{2}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(Y)
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
, such that

\mu_{n_{k}}=\nu_{k}^{1}+\nu_{k}^{2}\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad\nu_{k}^{i}\rightharpoonup\nu^{i}\quad\textrm{as }k\to+\infty,\,\quad i=1,2\,.

Proof.

Let us denote by
f\coloneqq\frac{{\mathrm{d}}\nu^{1}}{{\mathrm{d}}\mu}
the Radon–Nikodým derivative of
\nu^{1}
w.r.t.
\mu
and let
(f_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset{\mathrm{C}}_{b}(Y)
be such that
f_{k}\to f
in
L^{1}(\mu)
.
Up to replacing
f_{k}
with
f_{k}\wedge 1\vee 0
, we can assume without loss of generality that
0\leq f_{k}\leq 1
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
. By continuity and boundedness of
f_{k}
it holds
f_{k}\mu_{n}\rightharpoonup f_{k}\mu
as
n\to+\infty
and by construction
f_{k}\mu\rightharpoonup f\mu=\nu^{1}
as
k\to+\infty
, so that the metrizability of weak convergence on
\mathcal{M}_{+}(Y)
(cf.
[
6
, Theorem 5.1.3]
) and a diagonal argument provide us with a subsequence
(n_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
n_{k}\uparrow+\infty
and
\nu_{k}^{1}\coloneqq f_{k}\mu_{n_{k}}\rightharpoonup\nu^{1}
as
k\to+\infty
. As for
\nu^{2}
, it is sufficient to consider
\nu_{k}^{2}\coloneqq(1-f_{k})\mu_{n_{k}}
. The fact that
0\leq f_{k}\leq 1
ensures
\nu_{k}^{1},\nu_{k}^{2}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(Y)
. Moreover,
\mu_{n_{k}}=\nu_{k}^{1}+\nu_{k}^{2}
holds by construction, and
\nu_{k}^{2}=\mu_{n_{k}}-\nu_{k}^{1}\rightharpoonup\mu-\nu^{1}=\nu^{2}
as
k\to+\infty
.
∎

Finally, in the next result we show that if we have probability measures
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}},{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}
concentrated on curves with radii uniformly bounded away from zero and
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}\rightharpoonup{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}
, then a sort of discretized action (which is upper bounded by the energy
\mathcal{E}_{N_{k}}(P_{k})
, see (
7.5
) and (
7.45
)) controls from above in the limit the action
\mathcal{A}_{2}
, thus bridging between inf-convolution and
{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}
.

Proposition 7.6
.

Let
(X,{\sf d})
be a complete and separable metric space. For some
\varepsilon,R>0
let
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}},{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathrm{AC}^{2}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}_{R,\varepsilon}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}})))
, where
\mathfrak{C}_{R,\varepsilon}[X]
is as in (
2.6b
). Assume that
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}\rightharpoonup{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}
and
\sup_{k}\int\mathcal{A}_{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}=:C<+\infty
. Then

\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int\int_{0}^{1}u_{k}(\mathfrak{y},t)|\mathfrak{y}^{\prime}(t)|^{2}_{{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}}{\,\rm d}t{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}(\mathfrak{y})\geq\int\mathcal{A}_{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}\,,

where
\mathcal{A}_{2}
is as in (
4.9
) and
u_{k}
is defined as

(7.32)

u_{k}(\mathfrak{y},t)\coloneqq 1\wedge\frac{r_{\mathfrak{y}}(\left\lceil tN_{k}\right\rceil/N_{k})}{r_{\mathfrak{y}}(\left\lfloor tN_{k}\right\rfloor/N_{k})},\qquad\mathfrak{y}\in{\mathrm{C}}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}_{\Theta,\varepsilon}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}})),\,t\in[0,1]

for a sequence
\mathbb{N}\ni N_{k}\uparrow+\infty
.

Proof.

We set

\mathcal{A}_{2,k}(\mathfrak{y})\coloneqq\int_{0}^{1}u_{k}(\mathfrak{y},t)|\mathfrak{y}^{\prime}(t)|^{2}_{{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}}{\,\rm d}t,\qquad\mathfrak{y}\in\mathrm{AC}^{2}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}_{R,\varepsilon}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}))\,.

We start by proving the following claim:
for every compact subset
B\subset{\mathrm{C}}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}))
and every
\gamma>0
there exists
K=K(\varepsilon,B,\gamma)\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\left|\mathcal{A}_{2,k}(\mathfrak{y})-\mathcal{A}_{2}(\mathfrak{y})\right|<\gamma\mathcal{A}_{2}(\mathfrak{y})\,\text{ for every }\mathfrak{y}\in\mathrm{AC}^{2}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}_{R,\varepsilon}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}))\cap B,\,k\geq K.

To see this, we observe that, by compactness of
B
, for every
\eta>0
there exists a
\delta=\delta(\eta,B)>0
such that
|r_{\mathfrak{y}}(t)-r_{\mathfrak{y}}(t^{\prime})|\leq\eta
for every
t,t^{\prime}\in[0,1]
with
|t-t^{\prime}|<\delta
and for every
\mathfrak{y}\in B
. Choosing
\eta\coloneqq\gamma/\varepsilon
, we can find
K=K(\varepsilon,B,\gamma)\in\mathbb{N}
large enough such that
2/N_{k}\leq\delta(\eta,B)
whenever
k\geq K
. Hence, for
k\geq K
,
\mathfrak{y}\in\mathrm{AC}^{2}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}_{R,\varepsilon}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}))\cap B
and
t\in[0,1]
, we have

\left|1-u_{k}(\mathfrak{y},t)\right|\leq\left|1-\frac{r_{\mathfrak{y}}(\left\lceil tN_{k}\right\rceil/N_{k})}{r_{\mathfrak{y}}(\left\lfloor tN_{k}\right\rfloor/N_{k})}\right|\leq\frac{\left|r_{\mathfrak{y}}(\left\lfloor t/N_{k}\right\rfloor N_{k})-r_{\mathfrak{y}}(\left\lceil t/N_{k}\right\rceil N_{k})\right|}{\varepsilon}\leq\gamma

which also proves the claim.

Let us now conclude the proof of the proposition. Let
\gamma>0
be fixed. By tightness of
({\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k})_{k}
we can find a compact set
B_{\gamma}\subset{\mathrm{C}}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}))
such that
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}(B^{c}_{\gamma})<\gamma
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
. By the previous claim, we can find
K=K(\varepsilon,B_{\gamma},\gamma)\in N
such that

\left|\mathcal{A}_{2,k}(\mathfrak{y})-\mathcal{A}_{2}(\mathfrak{y})\right|<\gamma\mathcal{A}_{2}(\mathfrak{y})\quad\text{for every }\mathfrak{y}\in\mathrm{AC}^{2}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}_{R,\varepsilon}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}))\cap B_{\gamma},\,k\geq K\,.

We fix a threshold
m\in\mathbb{N}
and we obtain for every
k\geq K
that

\displaystyle\int\mathcal{A}_{2,k}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}

\displaystyle\geq\int\left(\mathcal{A}_{2,k}\wedge m\right){\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}=\int\left(\mathcal{A}_{2,k}\wedge m-\mathcal{A}_{2}\wedge m\right){\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}+\int\left(\mathcal{A}_{2}\wedge m\right){\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}

\displaystyle\geq-\int\left|\mathcal{A}_{2,k}\wedge m-\mathcal{A}_{2}\wedge m\right|{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}+\int\left(\mathcal{A}_{2}\wedge m\right){\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}

\displaystyle=-\int_{B_{\gamma}}\left|\mathcal{A}_{2,k}\wedge m-\mathcal{A}_{2}\wedge m\right|{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}-\int_{B_{\gamma}^{c}}\left|\mathcal{A}_{2,k}\wedge m-\mathcal{A}_{2}\wedge m\right|{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}+\int\left(\mathcal{A}_{2}\wedge m\right){\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}

\displaystyle\geq-\int_{B_{\gamma}}\left|\mathcal{A}_{2,k}-\mathcal{A}_{2}\right|{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}-2m\gamma+\int\left(\mathcal{A}_{2}\wedge m\right){\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}

\displaystyle\geq-\gamma\int_{B_{\gamma}}\mathcal{A}_{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}-2m\gamma+\int\left(\mathcal{A}_{2}\wedge m\right){\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}

\displaystyle\geq-\gamma(C+2m)+\int\left(\mathcal{A}_{2}\wedge m\right){\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}\,.

Passing to the limit inferior as
k\to+\infty
, using the weak convergence of
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}
to
\pi
and the lower semicontinuity of
\mathcal{A}_{2}
, we get

\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int\mathcal{A}_{2,k}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}_{k}\geq-\gamma(C+2m)+\int\left(\mathcal{A}_{2}\wedge m\right){\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}},

so that passing first to the limit as
\gamma\downarrow 0
and then to the limit as
m\uparrow+\infty
conclude the proof.
∎

7.3.
Proof of Theorem
7.1

Let us fix
\mu_{0},\mu_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(X)
and let us recall the definition of the measures
{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}
provided in (
7.4
). By Proposition
7.4
it is not restrictive to assume
(X,{\sf d})
to be a
compact
geodesic metric space. Moreover, from (
7.1
) and (
7.5
), we only need to prove

\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}\geq{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})\,.

As anticipated, this will be achieved in four steps.

1st step
. Let us define
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}\coloneqq\pi^{0,N_{k}}_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}
and assume, up to extracting a subsequence, that
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}\rightharpoonup{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}
as
k\to+\infty
. This is possible because all measures
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}
are supported on the compact set
\mathfrak{C}_{\Theta}[X]^{2}
(recall that
{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}
is supported in
\mathfrak{C}_{\Theta}[X]^{N_{k}+1}
with
\Theta
as in (
7.3
) and
X
is compact) and are probabilities, hence are tight. Then, let us decompose
\sigma
into
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{>}
and
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}
, i.e.
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}={\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{>}+{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}
where

{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{>}\coloneqq{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(\mathfrak{C}[X]\setminus\{\mathfrak{o}\}\times\mathfrak{C}[X]\setminus\{\mathfrak{o}\})\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}\coloneqq(\mathfrak{C}[X]\times\{\mathfrak{o}\}\cup\{\mathfrak{o}\}\times\mathfrak{C}[X])\,.

Up to extracting a further subsequence, by Lemma
7.5
there exist
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{>}^{k},{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}^{k}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\mathfrak{C}_{\Theta}[X]^{2})
such that
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}={\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{>}^{k}+{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}^{k}
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{>}^{k}\rightharpoonup{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{>}
and
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}^{k}\rightharpoonup{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}
as
k\to+\infty
. In order to decompose each
{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}
accordingly, if we disintegrate
{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}
w.r.t.
\pi^{0,N_{k}}
we obtain a family of probability measures
({\mbox{\boldmath$\eta$}}^{k}_{\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}}})_{\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}}\in\mathfrak{C}[X]}\subset\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N_{k}+1})
such that
{\mbox{\boldmath$\eta$}}^{k}_{\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}}}
is concentrated on
\{\mathfrak{y}_{0}\}\times\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N_{k}-1}\times\{\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}}\}
for
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}
-a.e.
(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}})\in\mathfrak{C}[X,X]
and

{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}=\int_{\mathfrak{C}[X,X]}{\mbox{\boldmath$\eta$}}^{k}_{\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}}}\,{\mathrm{d}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}})\,.

Hence, the natural way to decompose
{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}
is given by
{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}={\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{>}^{k}+{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}^{k}
with

{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{>}^{k}\coloneqq\int_{\mathfrak{C}[X,X]}{\mbox{\boldmath$\eta$}}^{k}_{\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}}}\,{\mathrm{d}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{>}^{k}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}})\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}^{k}\coloneqq\int_{\mathfrak{C}[X,X]}{\mbox{\boldmath$\eta$}}^{k}_{\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}}}\,{\mathrm{d}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}^{k}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}})\,,

as in such a way
\pi^{0,N_{k}}_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{>}^{k}={\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{>}^{k}
and
\pi^{0,N_{k}}_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}^{k}={\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}^{k}
. Let us further split the measure
{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{>}^{k}
into

{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{g}\coloneqq{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{>}^{k}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\mathcal{D}_{N_{k}}^{c}\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{b}\coloneqq{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{>}^{k}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\mathcal{D}_{N_{k}}\,,

where we recall

\mathcal{D}_{N_{k}}=\left\{\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}<\tilde{{\sf d}}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{2}\circ\pi^{0,N_{k}}\right\}\subset\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N_{k}+1}\,,

see also (
7.21
) and (
7.22
).
Let us observe, for later use, that if
(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}})\in\mathcal{D}_{N_{k}}
, then
{\sf r}(\mathfrak{y}_{0})>0
and
{\sf r}(\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}})>0
, as shown at the beginning of the proof of Theorem
7.3
. This implies that
{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k}
is actually concentrated on

(7.33)

\left\{(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}})\,:\,\begin{array}[]{c}({\sf r}(\mathfrak{y}_{0}),\dots,{\sf r}(\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}}))\in(0,\Theta]\times[0,\Theta]^{N_{k}-1}\times(0,\Theta]\\[10.0pt]
\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}})<\tilde{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{2}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}})\end{array}\right\}\subset\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N_{k}+1}\,.

As already remarked for
({\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, also
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}_{g}\coloneqq\pi^{0,N_{k}}_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{g}
and
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}_{b}\coloneqq\pi^{0,N_{k}}_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{b}
are tight families of measures. We can thus assume that, up to extracting subsequences,
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}_{x}
and their 2-homogeneous marginals
\mu_{x,0}^{k}\coloneqq\mathfrak{h}_{0}^{2}({\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}_{x})
,
\mu_{x,1}^{k}\coloneqq\mathfrak{h}_{1}^{2}({\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}_{x})
converge weakly to some
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{x}\in\mathfrak{H}^{2}(\mu_{x,0},\mu_{x,1})
,
\mu_{x,0}
, and
\mu_{x,1}
respectively as
k\to+\infty
, where
x\in\{g,b,\mathfrak{o}\}
. As a consequence,

(7.34)

\mu_{g,0}+\mu_{b,0}+\mu_{\mathfrak{o},0}=\mu_{0}\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad\mu_{g,1}+\mu_{b,1}+\mu_{\mathfrak{o},1}=\mu_{1}\,.

Indeed, by construction for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
it holds

\mu_{g,i}^{k}+\mu_{b,i}^{k}+\mu_{\mathfrak{o},i}^{k}=\mathfrak{h}^{2}_{i}({\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}_{g}+{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}_{b}+{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}^{k})=\mathfrak{h}_{i}^{2}(\pi^{0,N_{k}}_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k})=\mu_{i},\qquad i=0,1\,.

After this premise, note that by Jensen’s inequality

\begin{split}\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{\mathfrak{o}}&=N_{k}\int\sum_{i=1}^{N_{k}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{W}\mathsf{He}}(\mathfrak{y}_{i-1},\mathfrak{y}_{i}){\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{\mathfrak{o}}\geq N_{k}\int\sum_{i=1}^{N_{k}}|r_{i-1}-r_{i}|^{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{\mathfrak{o}}\\
&\geq\int\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N_{k}}|r_{i-1}-r_{i}|\right)^{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{\mathfrak{o}}\geq\int|r_{0}-r_{N_{k}}|^{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{\mathfrak{o}}\\
&=\int_{\mathfrak{C}[X,X]}|r_{0}-r_{1}|^{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}_{\mathfrak{o}}\,,\end{split}

so that

\begin{split}\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{\mathfrak{o}}&\geq\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int_{\mathfrak{C}[X,X]}|r_{0}-r_{1}|^{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}_{\mathfrak{o}}=\int_{\mathfrak{C}[X,X]}|r_{0}-r_{1}|^{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}\\
&=\int_{\mathfrak{C}[X,X]}{\sf d}_{\pi/2,\mathfrak{C}}^{2}\,{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}\geq{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mu_{\mathfrak{o},0},\mu_{\mathfrak{o},1})\,,\end{split}

where the last identity comes from the definition of the cone distance (
2.7
) in the particular case when one of the two points is the vertex: note indeed that by construction
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{\mathfrak{o}}
is concentrated on points like
(\mathfrak{o},\mathfrak{y})
or
(\mathfrak{y},\mathfrak{o})
, so that either
r_{0}
or
r_{1}
is equal to zero.

Moreover, on the ‘good’ set
\mathcal{D}_{N_{k}}^{c}
we easily get the following lower bound

\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{g}\geq\int_{\mathfrak{C}[X,X]}\tilde{{\sf d}}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}_{g}\geq\int_{\mathfrak{C}[X,X]}{\sf d}_{\pi/2,\mathfrak{C}}^{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}^{k}_{g}\geq{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mu_{g,0}^{k},\mu_{g,1}^{k})\,,

where
\mathsf{d}_{\pi/2,\mathfrak{C}}
was defined in (
2.7
) and, as pointed out right after (
7.23
), it is dominated by
\tilde{{\sf d}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
. Therefore, if we are able to show that

(7.35)

\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{b}\geq{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mu_{b,0},\mu_{b,1})\,,

then the conclusion immediately follows observing that

\displaystyle\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}

\displaystyle\geq\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{g}+\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{b}+\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{\mathfrak{o}}

\displaystyle\geq{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mu_{g,0},\mu_{g,1})+{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mu_{b,0},\mu_{b,1})+{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mu_{\mathfrak{o},0},\mu_{\mathfrak{o},1})\geq{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\mu_{0},\mu_{1})\,,

where the second inequality relies on the weak lower semicontinuity of
{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}
together with
\mu_{g,i}^{k}\rightharpoonup\mu_{g,i}
as
k\to+\infty
for
i=0,1
; the last inequality exploits (
7.34
) and the subadditivity of
{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}
(see Theorem
4.3
(2)). For this reason, let us now focus on (
7.35
).

2nd step.
For every
m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, let us consider an increasing function
f_{m}\in{\mathrm{C}}^{\infty}([0,+\infty))
such that

f_{m}\equiv 0\text{ in }[0,1/m]\,,\qquad f_{m}\equiv 1\text{ in }[2/m,+\infty)\,,

and let us set

{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k,m}_{b}\coloneqq(f_{m}\circ{\sf r}\circ\pi^{0})(f_{m}\circ{\sf r}\circ\pi^{N_{k}}){\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k}\,.

As preliminary considerations, note that
{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{b}\geq{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k,m}_{b}
, whence

(7.36)

\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{b}\geq\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}\,,\qquad\forall k,m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}\,.

It is also easy to check that
{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}
is concentrated on

(7.37)

\left\{(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}})\,:\,\begin{array}[]{c}({\sf r}(\mathfrak{y}_{0}),\dots,{\sf r}(\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}}))\in[m^{-1},\Theta]\times[0,\Theta]^{N_{k}-1}\times[m^{-1},\Theta]\\[10.0pt]
\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}})<\tilde{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{2}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}})\end{array}\right\}\subset\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N_{k}+1}\,.

Moreover, if we introduce
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{b}^{k,m}\coloneqq\pi_{\sharp}^{0,N_{k}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}
and its 2-homogneous marginals

\nu_{0}^{k,m}\coloneqq\mathfrak{h}^{2}(\pi^{0}_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}),\qquad\nu_{1}^{k,m}\coloneqq\mathfrak{h}^{2}(\pi^{N_{k}}_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m})\,,

it is not difficult to see that, as
k\to+\infty
, they converge weakly to measures

\bar{{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}}_{b}^{m}\coloneqq(f_{m}\circ{\sf r}\circ\pi^{0})(f_{m}\circ{\sf r}\circ\pi^{1}){\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{b}

and

\nu_{0}^{m}\coloneqq\mathfrak{h}^{2}(\pi^{0}_{\sharp}\bar{{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}}_{b}^{m})\,,\qquad\nu_{1}^{m}\coloneqq\mathfrak{h}^{2}(\pi^{1}_{\sharp}\bar{{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}}_{b}^{m})

respectively, satisfying
\bar{{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}}_{b}^{m}\rightharpoonup{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{b}
and
\nu_{i}^{m}\rightharpoonup\mu_{b,i}
,
i=0,1
, as
m\to+\infty
. Indeed, by construction
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{b}
does not charge the set
\mathfrak{C}[X]\times\{\mathfrak{o}\}\cup\{\mathfrak{o}\}\times\mathfrak{C}[X]
, so that
(f_{m}\circ{\sf r}\circ\pi^{0})(f_{m}\circ{\sf r}\circ\pi^{1})\to 1

{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{b}
-a.e. as
m\to+\infty
. Since
|f_{m}|\leq 1
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, by Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem we deduce that, for every
\varphi\in{\mathrm{C}}_{b}(\mathfrak{C}[X,X])
,

\lim_{m\to+\infty}\int_{\mathfrak{C}[X,X]}\varphi\,{\,\rm d}\bar{{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}}_{b}^{m}=\lim_{m\to+\infty}\int_{\mathfrak{C}[X,X]}\varphi\,(f_{m}\circ{\sf r}\circ\pi^{0})(f_{m}\circ{\sf r}\circ\pi^{1})\,{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{b}=\int_{\mathfrak{C}[X,X]}\varphi\,{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{b}\,,

as desired.

We now claim that it is sufficient to prove that

(7.38)

\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}\geq{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\nu_{0}^{m},\nu_{1}^{m})\,,\qquad\forall m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}\,.

Indeed, by (
7.36
) and (
7.38
) we have

\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}_{b}\geq\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}\geq{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\nu_{0}^{m},\nu_{1}^{m})\,,\qquad\forall m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}\,,

so that passing to the limit as
m\to+\infty
and using the convergence of
\nu_{i}^{m}
to
\mu_{b,i}
, for
i=0,1
, together with the joint weak lower semicontinuity of
{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}
lead to (
7.35
).

3rd step.
Thus, we fix
m\in\mathbb{N}
and we devote the remaining part of the proof to show (
7.38
), passing first of all to a non-relabeled subsequence such that the limit inferior in (
7.38
) is achieved as a limit. Second, we observe that the weak convergence of
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{b}^{k,m}
towards
\bar{{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}}_{b}^{m}
implies the convergence of the masses:

{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{b}^{k,m}(\mathfrak{C}[X,X])\to\bar{{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}}_{b}^{m}(\mathfrak{C}[X,X])\qquad\textrm{as }k\to+\infty\,.

If
\bar{{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}}_{b}^{m}(\mathfrak{C}[X,X])=0
, then
\nu_{0}^{m}
and
\nu_{1}^{m}
are both the null measure and in this case (
7.38
) trivially holds. We can thus assume
\bar{{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}}_{b}^{m}(\mathfrak{C}[X,X])>0
. This implies that (up to passing to a subsequence) the mass of all
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{b}^{k,m}
is bounded from below by a strictly positive constant, uniformly in
k
. As on the other hand
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{b}^{k,m}(\mathfrak{C}[X,X])\leq 1
(because
{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{b}^{k,m}
is obtained via restriction/cut-off of the probability measure
{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}
), we conclude that there exists a constant
c_{m}\in(1,+\infty)
such that

(7.39)

1\leq c_{k,m}\coloneqq\left({\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{b}^{k,m}(\mathfrak{C}[X,X])\right)^{-1/2}\leq c_{m}\,,\qquad\forall k\in\mathbb{N}\,.

We now make use of Theorem
7.3
to modify
{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}
: leveraging the map
G_{N_{k}}
introduced therein we define a new plan

\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}\coloneqq(G_{N_{k}})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}

and note that
\pi^{0,N_{k}}_{\sharp}\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}\in\mathfrak{H}^{2}(\nu_{0}^{k,m},\nu_{1}^{k,m})
, because of
\pi^{0}\circ G_{N_{k}}=\pi^{0}
and
\pi^{N_{k}}\circ G_{N_{k}}=\pi^{N_{k}}
, see (
7.24
). Using the fact that
H_{N_{k}}\circ G_{N_{k}}\leq H_{N_{k}}
, again by (
7.24
), we also remark that

(7.40)

\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}\geq\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}\,.

Finally, observe that
\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}
is concentrated on

\left\{(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}})\,:\,\begin{array}[]{c}({\sf r}(\mathfrak{y}_{0}),\dots,{\sf r}(\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}}))\in\left[(5m)^{-1},\Theta\right]^{N_{k}+1}\\[10.0pt]
{\sf d}({\sf x}(\mathfrak{y}_{i-1}),{\sf x}(\mathfrak{y}_{i}))\in\left[0,\frac{8\Theta m}{\sqrt{N_{k}}}\right]\,,\quad i=1,\dots,N_{k}\end{array}\right\}\,.

Indeed, the fact that
{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}
is concentrated on (
7.37
) allows to apply the radial and spatial quantitative bounds (
7.25
) and (
7.26
). For ease of computations, we slightly weaken them, observing that
\frac{1}{5}<1-\frac{\pi}{4}
and
\frac{\pi}{2-\pi/2}<8
. Then, since
\Theta
and
m
are fixed, up to choosing
k
sufficiently large we can assume that
{\sf d}({\sf x}(\mathfrak{y}_{i-1}),{\sf x}(\mathfrak{y}_{i}))\in[0,1]
for every
i=1,\dots,N_{k}
.

As next step, we apply to
\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}
a dilation of parameter
c_{k,m}
(with
c_{k,m}
defined in (
7.39
), see also (
2.15
) for the definition of
\text{dil}_{c_{k,m},2}
):

\hat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}\coloneqq\text{dil}_{c_{k,m},2}(\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}).

This further measure is concentrated on

\left\{(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}})\,:\,\begin{array}[]{c}({\sf r}(\mathfrak{y}_{0}),\dots,{\sf r}(\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}}))\in\left[(5mc_{m})^{-1},\Theta\right]^{N_{k}+1}\\[10.0pt]
{\sf d}({\sf x}(\mathfrak{y}_{i-1}),{\sf x}(\mathfrak{y}_{i}))\in\left[0,1\right]\,,\quad i=1,\dots,N_{k}\end{array}\right\}\,,

since
1\leq c_{k,m}\leq c_{m}
by (
7.39
) and dilations only act radially: this implies that the conditions
{\sf d}({\sf x}(\mathfrak{y}_{i-1}),{\sf x}(\mathfrak{y}_{i}))\in[0,1]
,
i=1,\dots,N_{k}
, valid in the support of
\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}
still hold in the support of
\hat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}
. Moreover, let us observe that
\hat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N_{k}+1})
, because

\hat{{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}}_{b}^{k,m}(\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N_{k}+1})=\int c_{k,m}^{2}{\,\rm d}\tilde{{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}}_{b}^{k,m}=\int_{\mathfrak{C}[X,X]}c_{k,m}^{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\sigma$}}_{b}^{k,m}=1\,,

and
\pi^{0,N_{k}}_{\sharp}\hat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}\in\mathfrak{H}^{2}(\nu_{0}^{k,m},\nu_{1}^{k,m})
, since homogeneous marginals are invariant w.r.t. dilations, see (
2.16
). By (
2.17
) we also have

\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}=\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}\hat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}

and combining this identity with (
7.40
) yields

(7.41)

\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}\geq\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}\tilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}=\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}\hat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}\,.

In view of (
7.38
), it is thus sufficient to prove

(7.42)

\liminf_{k\to+\infty}\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}\hat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}\geq{\mathsf{H}\!\!\mathsf{K}}^{2}(\nu_{0}^{m},\nu_{1}^{m}),\qquad\forall m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1},

and this is the purpose of the fourth and final step of the proof.

4th step.
Let us now lift
\hat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}
to the path space. To this end, let us define the geodesic interpolation map
\Gamma^{N_{k}}:\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N_{k}+1}\to\mathrm{AC}^{2}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}))
as

(7.43)

\begin{split}\Gamma^{N_{k}}(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}})(t)\coloneqq\Gamma^{X}(\mathfrak{y}_{i-1},\mathfrak{y}_{i})(N_{k}t-(i-1)),\qquad\textrm{if}\quad&\frac{i-1}{N_{k}}\leq t\leq\frac{i}{N_{k}}\\[5.0pt]
&i=1,\dots,N_{k},\end{split}

where
\Gamma^{X}
is the
\mathcal{B}(X\times X)^{*}
-
\mathcal{B}({\mathrm{C}}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}})))
measurable geodesic selection map constructed in Theorem
A.2
. Then

{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k,m}\coloneqq\Gamma^{N_{k}}_{\sharp}\hat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathrm{AC}^{2}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}})))\,,

and note that by construction and by Remark
2.1
the plan
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k,m}
is concentrated on piecewise geodesic curves taking values in
\mathfrak{C}_{\Theta,\varepsilon_{m}}[X]
(cf. (
2.6b
)), i.e.
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k,m}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathrm{AC}^{2}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}_{\Theta,\varepsilon_{m}}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}})))
, where

\varepsilon_{m}\coloneqq\frac{1}{5\sqrt{2}mc_{m}}\,.

Indeed, the fact that
\hat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}
is concentrated on points
(\mathfrak{y}_{0},\dots,\mathfrak{y}_{N_{k}})\in\mathfrak{C}[X]^{N_{k}+1}
with
{\sf r}(\mathfrak{y}_{i})\geq(5mc_{m})^{-1}>0
for all
i=0,\dots,N_{k}
and
{\sf d}({\sf x}(\mathfrak{y}_{i-1}),{\sf x}(\mathfrak{y}_{i}))\leq 1\leq\pi/2
for all
i=1,\dots,N_{k}
ensures, by (
2.13
), that the minimal radius of any constant speed geodesic connecting
\mathfrak{y}_{i-1}
and
\mathfrak{y}_{i}
is larger than
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}{\sf r}(\mathfrak{y}_{i-1})\wedge{\sf r}(\mathfrak{y}_{i})\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(5mc_{m})^{-1}=\varepsilon_{m}
.

Recalling the definition (
4.9
) of
\mathcal{A}_{2}
, we also have

(7.44)

\begin{split}\int\mathcal{A}_{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k,m}&=\int N_{k}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{k}}{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{2}({\sf e}_{(i-1)/N_{k}},{\sf e}_{i/N_{k}}){\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k,m}\\
&=\int N_{k}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{k}}{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{2}(\pi^{i-1},\pi^{i}){\,\rm d}\hat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}\leq 2\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}\hat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}\leq 2\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k,m}\\
&\leq 2\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}_{b}^{k}\leq 2\int\mathsf{H}_{N_{k}}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{k}=2\mathcal{E}_{N_{k}}(P_{k})\leq 2C<+\infty\,,\end{split}

where we have used, in order, the definition of
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k,m}
, Lemma
5.4
, (
7.41
), (
7.36
), (
7.5
), and (
7.2
). This fact together with the tightness of
\{({\sf e}_{t})_{\sharp}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k,m}\,:\,t\in[0,1],\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}\subset\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{C}_{\Theta}[X])
(immediate consequence of the compactness of
\mathfrak{C}_{\Theta}[X]
) entail, by
[
1
, Theorem 10.4]
, that
({\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k,m})_{k}
is tight. Hence, up to passing to a subsequence,
({\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{k,m})_{k}
weakly converges as
k\to+\infty
to some
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{m}\in\mathcal{P}({\mathrm{C}}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}})))
, but a more accurate statement actually holds.

Indeed, the lower semicontinuity of
\mathcal{A}_{2}
w.r.t. weak convergence together with (
7.44
) implies that
\int\mathcal{A}_{2}{\,\rm d}{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{m}<+\infty
, namely
{\mbox{\boldmath$\pi$}}^{m}
is concentrated on
\mathrm{AC}^{2}([0,1];(\mathfrak{C}[X],{\sf d}_{\mathfrak{C}}))
